Pseudouridine synthases (Ψ synthases) catalyze the isomerization of uridine to pseudouridine (5-ribosyluracil) in RNA. This is a C-C bond formation reaction where the C5 of uracil forms a new bond to the C1&#x27; of ribose.

The mechanism involves a nucleophilic attack. There are two proposed mechanisms:
1. In archaeal/eukaryotic Ψ synthases (TruB family), a cysteine residue acts as the nucleophile that attacks C6 of uracil, forming a covalent intermediate.
2. In bacterial TruB, a cysteine is also the nucleophile.

However, for RluA family pseudouridine synthases (which are bacterial), a different mechanism is proposed where an aspartate residue acts as the nucleophile.
